. Recall that the map
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{t}_{P}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
is remembering the composition

\overline{\kappa}^{\mathcal{V}}:(\mathcal{V}^{[P,P]})_{\mathcal{F}_{M^{\mathrm{ab}}}^{2}}\xrightarrow{\beta^{\mathcal{V}^{[P,P]}}_{M^{\mathrm{ab}}}}(\mathcal{V}^{[P,P]})_{\mathcal{F}_{M^{\mathrm{ab}}}^{1}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{G}^{1}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,(\beta^{\mathcal{V}})^{-1}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,(\beta^{\mathcal{V}})^{-1}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\,(\beta^{\mathcal{V}})^{-1}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,(\beta^{\mathcal{V}})^{-1}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}}\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{G}}

for all
G
-modules
\mathcal{V}
. Moreover, since
\phantom{}{}_{\theta^{\prime}}\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}
maps to
\phantom{}{}_{\theta^{\prime}}\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
, by definition, we have that the cokernel of the map
\overline{\kappa}^{\mathcal{V}}
has torsion of length equal to
\langle\mathcal{V}^{[P,P]},\theta^{\prime}\rangle
.

On the other hand, by point (5) in the definition of
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}
, we can compose
\overline{\kappa}^{\mathcal{V}}
with the well-defined surjective map

\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{G}}\xrightarrow{\beta^{\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{G}^{1}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\kappa^{-,\mathcal{V}}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\kappa^{-,\mathcal{V}}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\,\kappa^{-,\mathcal{V}}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,\kappa^{-,\mathcal{V}}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}}(\mathcal{V}_{U^{-}})_{\mathcal{F}_{M}^{1}}.

If we start with the
M^{\mathrm{ab}}
-module
\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{V}^{[P,P]}
and set
\mathcal{V}=H^{0}(G/P^{-},\mathcal{W})
, there is a natural map
\mathcal{V}_{U^{-}}\to\mathcal{W}
and we get the composite map

\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}_{M^{\mathrm{ab}}}^{2}}\xrightarrow{\beta^{\mathcal{W}}_{M^{\mathrm{ab}}}}\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}_{M^{\mathrm{ab}}}^{1}}\to(\mathcal{V}_{U^{-}})_{\mathcal{F}_{M}^{1}}\to\mathcal{W}_{\mathcal{F}_{M}^{1}}

which is really just the injective map
\beta^{\mathcal{W}}_{M^{\mathrm{ab}}}
, and has cokernel given by a torsion bundle of length
\langle\mathcal{W},\theta\rangle
. The torsion part of the cokernel of
\overline{\kappa}^{\mathcal{V}}
injects into the cokernel of this composite map. Thus,

\langle\mathcal{V}^{[P,P]},\theta^{\prime}\rangle\leq\langle\mathcal{W},\theta\rangle=\langle\mathcal{V}^{[P,P]},\theta\rangle

for all
G
-modules
\mathcal{V}
, which implies
\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta
.

If
\theta=\theta^{\prime}
, we can saturate
\overline{\kappa}^{\mathcal{V}}
to get a well-defined map

(\mathcal{V}^{[P,P]})_{\mathcal{F}_{M^{\mathrm{ab}}}^{1}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\mathcal{F}_{G}}

whose cokernel is a vector bundle, yielding a
P
-reduction of
\mathcal{F}_{G}
that is transverse to the
P^{-}
-reduction; thus
\mathcal{F}_{G}=\mathcal{F}_{G}^{1}
.
∎

It would be very nice to say that
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}
surjects onto the locus
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
. However, this is not true. In particular, it only uniformizes the locus of
\phantom{}{}_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
coming from split reductions, as one easily verifies from the construction.

Lemma 5.1.10
.

The composition

\mathfrak{s}^{\theta}:\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\phantom{}_{\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\rightarrow\phantom{}_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}\simeq\mathrm{Bun}_{P}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}

is given by the map
\alpha\times\mathrm{id}
, where

\alpha:\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\xrightarrow{h_{M}^{\rightarrow}}\mathrm{Bun}_{M}\rightarrow\mathrm{Bun}_{P},

and the last map is the section of
\mathfrak{q}_{P}
given by sending
\mathcal{F}_{M}
to
\mathcal{F}_{M}\times^{M}P
.

Fortunately, after trivializing the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{M}^{1}
, we can arrange cohomologically smooth locally that
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}
uniformizes
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
. To see this, we will need to use the factorization structure on the Zastava spaces.

5.2.
Factorization

In this section, we will stick to studying the Zastava space
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}
, which we recall is the space studied in the previous section, but the bundle
\mathcal{F}_{M}^{1}
is equal to the trivial
M
-bundle. This space maps naturally to

\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}:=\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\ast.

These spaces exhibit a nice factorization structure.

In particular, let
\theta=\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}
with
\theta_{i}\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
. We write

(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}\subset\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})}

for the open subset where the corresponding Cartier divisors are disjoint. We note that there is a natural étale map

(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}

given by taking the union of Cartier divisors. We have the following factorization property.

Proposition 5.2.1
.

With notation as above, there is a natural isomorphism

(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\simeq(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})}}(\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{1}}\times\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{2}})

lying over a natural isomorphism

(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\simeq(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})}}(\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta_{1}}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta_{2}}).

such that the sections
\mathfrak{s}^{\theta}
and
\mathfrak{s}^{\theta_{1}}\times\mathfrak{s}^{\theta_{2}}
agree after base-changing and applying these identifications.

Proof.

For
S\in\mathrm{Perf}
, consider an
S
-point of
(\mathcal{F}_{G},\mathcal{F}_{M},\beta_{M},\beta)
of
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})
lying over the open subspace
(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}
, and let
D_{S}^{1}
and
D_{S}^{2}
be the effective Cartier divisors in
X_{S}
corresponding to
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
, respectively. We let
X_{S}\setminus D_{S}^{i}=:X_{S}^{i}
for
i=1,2
, and set
X_{S}^{0}:=X_{S}\setminus(\bigcup_{i=1}^{2}D_{S}^{i})
. For
i=1,2
, by using Beauville-Laszlo gluing
[
SW20
, Lemma 5.2.9]
on
X_{S}
we can define
G
-bundles
\mathcal{F}_{G}^{i}
by modifying the trivial
G
-bundle via
\beta
at just the formal completions of the
D_{S}^{i}
. By construction, we have modifications
\beta^{i}:\mathcal{F}_{G}^{i}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{G}^{0}
which are isomorphisms away from the
D_{i}
. Similarly, from the
S
-point
(\mathcal{F}_{M},\beta_{M})
in
\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}
, we obtain
S
-points
(\mathcal{F}_{M}^{i},\beta_{M}^{i})\in\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta_{i}}_{M}
for
i=1,2
, and in turn points
(\mathcal{F}_{G}^{i},\mathcal{F}_{M}^{i},\beta_{M}^{i},\beta^{i})\in\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{i}}
, as desired. The converse direction proceeds analogously.
∎

To simplify the notation, if we are given a stack
Z
living over
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})}
, we write
Z_{\mathrm{disj}}
for its pullback to the open subspace
(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}
so that the factorization property reads
(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\simeq(Z^{\tilde{\theta}_{1}}\times Z^{\tilde{\theta}_{2}})_{\mathrm{disj}}
.

Fix an element
\theta^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
. As in the previous section, the space
\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}
has a stratification given by
\phantom{}{}_{\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}
for
\theta\leq\theta^{\prime}
given by fixing the torsion cokernel of the embeddings

(\mathcal{V}^{U})_{\mathcal{F}_{M}}\xrightarrow{\beta^{\mathcal{V}^{U}}_{M}}(\mathcal{V}^{U})_{\mathcal{F}_{M}^{0}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{V}^{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathcal{F}_{G}^{0}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,(\beta^{\mathcal{V}})^{-1}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,(\beta^{\mathcal{V}})^{-1}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\,(\beta^{\mathcal{V}})^{-1}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}{\mathrel{}\mathrel{\mathop{\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@\dabar@}\limits^{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\,(\beta^{\mathcal{V}})^{-1}$}}}\mathrel{\mathchar 0\symAMSa 04B}{}}}\mathcal{V}^{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathcal{F}_{G}},

where the open stratum corresponding to
\theta=0
is precisely
Z^{\theta^{\prime}}
.

Similarly, we write
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}
for the corresponding open subspaces given by bounding the torsion by
\theta
. If we are given a partition
\theta_{1}^{\prime}+\theta_{2}^{\prime}=\theta^{\prime}
then we write
(\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}\times\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{2}^{\prime}})_{\mathrm{disj},\leq\theta}\subset(\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}\times\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{2}^{\prime}})_{\mathrm{disj}}
for the open subspace corresponding to
(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{1})}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta_{2})})_{\mathrm{disj}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}
under the factorization isomorphism. One easily sees the following Corollary.

Corollary 5.2.2
.

For
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
with
\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta
, and a partition
\theta^{\prime}=\theta^{\prime}_{1}+\theta^{\prime}_{2}
, the subspace
(\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}\times\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{2}^{\prime}})_{\mathrm{disj},\leq\theta}\subset(\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}\times\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{2}^{\prime}})_{\mathrm{disj}}
is stratified by

(\phantom{}_{\theta_{1}}\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{1}^{\prime}}\times\phantom{}_{\theta_{2}}\tilde{Z}^{\theta_{2}^{\prime}})_{\mathrm{disj}}

for
\theta_{1}\leq\theta_{1}^{\prime}
and
\theta_{2}\leq\theta_{2}^{\prime}
such that
\theta_{1}+\theta_{2}=\theta
.

We will make use of the following related result.

Corollary 5.2.3
.

For
\theta,\theta^{\prime}\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
with
\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta
, we have an open immersion

(\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta})_{\mathrm{disj}}\hookrightarrow(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)})_{\mathrm{disj}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime})}}\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}

The factorization property is an expression of the fact that the Zastava space
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}
is really determined by local information around the corresponding point in
\mathrm{Div}^{\theta}
. One consequence of this "locality" is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on the Zastava space, studied in the next section.

5.3.
Hyperbolic localization on Zastava spaces

In §
5.1
, we saw that the map

h_{M}^{\rightarrow}:\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}

admits a section
\mathfrak{s}^{\theta}
. In this section, we construct (étale locally on
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
) a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}
which contracts to this section.
Consider
\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
, the
B_{dR}^{+}
Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian over
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
, as in
[
FS21
, Definition VI.1.8]
defined using the loop groups
L^{+}_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}G
and
L_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}G
. For
d\geq 0
, we will also consider the mirror curves

\mathrm{Div}^{(d)}_{\mathcal{Y}}:=\mathop{\rm Spd}(\breve{E})^{d}/S_{d},

which, for
S\in\mathrm{Perf}
, parametrizes Cartier divisors in the open adic Fargues-Fontaine curve
\mathcal{Y}_{S}
, by
[
FS21
]
.
7
7

7

Our notation here differs from that in
[
FS21
]
. In particular, what we denote as
\mathcal{Y}_{S}
is denoted there as
Y_{S}
; we emphasize that we do not include the locus where
\pi=0
.
Similarly, for a finite extension
E^{\prime}/E
, we write
\mathrm{Div}^{(d)}_{\mathcal{Y},E^{\prime}}
for the corresponding object constructed from
E^{\prime}
.

We recall that we have an étale covering (in fact a local isomorphism)
\mathcal{Y}_{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{S}
which in turn induces a map

\mathrm{Div}^{(d)}_{\mathcal{Y}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(d)}

of mirror curves that is étale locally split. More precisely, restricting to the open subspace
\mathrm{Div}^{(d),\prime}
of
\mathrm{Div}^{(d)}_{\mathcal{Y}}
where no point is a proper Frobenius translate of another point, the map to
\mathrm{Div}^{(d)}
is étale.

For
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
with
\theta=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{G,P}}n_{i}\alpha_{i}
, we set

\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}:=\prod_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{G,P}}\mathrm{Div}^{(n_{i}),\prime}_{\mathcal{Y},E_{i}}\supset\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}:=\prod_{i\in\mathcal{J}_{G,P}}\mathrm{Div}^{(n_{i}),\prime}_{E_{i}},

where
E_{i}
denotes the reflex field of the Galois orbit of simple coroots corresponding to
i\in\mathcal{J}_{G,P}
. Thus,
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}\subset\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}
is an open subset with an étale surjective map to
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
.

We write
\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}
for the associated Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian given as the étale sheafification of the quotient:
L_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}G/L^{+}_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}G
.

We choose a cocharacter
\lambda:\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow G
such that the associated parabolic
P_{\lambda}
under the dynamical method is
P^{-}
. We get an action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
by considering the composition

\mathbb{G}_{m}\rightarrow L^{+}_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\xrightarrow{L^{+}\lambda}L^{+}_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}G

and using the left action of
L^{+}_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
. Here the first map is given by the Teichmüller character, where we note that this is only well-defined on the curve
\mathcal{Y}_{S}
and not on the curve
X_{S}
, since on the latter we quotient out the relevant Witt vector ring by Frobenius.

We let
\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
) denote the Beilinson-Drinfeld affine Grassmannian attached to
U^{-}
over
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}
(resp.
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
). We recall that the projection map has a unit section corresponding to the trivial
L^{+}_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}U^{-}
(resp .
L^{+}_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}U^{-}
) orbit.

Proposition 5.3.1
.

The natural inclusion
\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
is a locally closed immersion. For
\lambda
as above, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
preserves the locally closed subspace
\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
and extends to an action of the multiplicative monoid
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. The
\mathbb{G}_{m}
fixed points of this action is the closed image of the unit section
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
.

Proof.

By
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.3.1]
, its proof, and our assumption on
\lambda
, we have that the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
preserves the subspace
\mathrm{Gr}_{P^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\subset\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
, which is a disjoint union of locally closed subspaces; the induced action follows by taking loop groups of the associated action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
P
by conjugation. Moreover, it extends to an action of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
such that its fixed points are
\mathrm{Gr}_{M,\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
. It follows by
[
SW20
, Proposition 20.3.7]
and the fact that
U^{-}
is preserved under conjugation by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
that
\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\subset\mathrm{Gr}_{G,\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
is also a locally closed subspace, which is preserved under a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action that extends to
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Its fixed points are precisely the unit section, by virtue of the fact that the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
U^{-}
by conjugation contracts to the identity.
∎

The Grassmannian
\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}
will parametrize modifications of
G
-bundles
\beta:\mathcal{F}_{G}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{G}^{0}
satisfying condition (5) of Definition
5.1.1
. Therefore, we have a natural map

\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}

mapping to the locus of the right-hand side where condition (4) of Definition
5.1.1
is satisfied. It follows that this is a closed subspace by an application of Lemma
4.1.4
. Moreover, under this embedding the section
\mathfrak{s}^{\theta}
is given by the base-change of the unit section of
\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
.

Since the map
\mathcal{Y}_{S}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}_{S}
is a local isomorphism, it induces an isomorphism on formal completions at Cartier divisors as long as no two distinct points of the Cartier divisor on
\mathcal{Y}_{S}
map to the same point of
\mathcal{X}_{S}
. This is enforced on
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}\subset\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}
, so it follows that we have a Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}}\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}
,

where the vertical maps are étale. If we write
\tilde{Z}^{\theta,\prime}
for the base-change of
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}
along the etale covering
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
then we obtain a closed subspace

\tilde{Z}^{\theta,\prime}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}.

On the target, there is the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action constructed above. We claim that this preserves
\tilde{Z}^{\theta,\prime}
, as well as the strata
\phantom{}{}_{\theta^{\prime}}\tilde{Z}^{\theta,\prime}
. To see this, note that
\tilde{Z}^{\theta,\prime}(S)
is defined functorially in the triple
(G\times_{E}\mathcal{Y}_{S},P\times_{E}\mathcal{Y}_{S},P^{-}\times_{E}\mathcal{Y}_{S})
, and conjugation by
[\lambda]
defines an action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on this triple. It is this action that gets transported through all structures, yielding the action both on
\mathrm{Gr}_{U^{-},\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}
(trivially on the second factor, as
\lambda
is central in
M
) and on
\tilde{Z}^{\theta,\prime}
, with its intrinsically defined stratification.

Corollary 5.3.2
.

After base-changing along the étale cover
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
, there exists a strata-preserving
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\tilde{Z}^{\theta,\prime}
which extends to an action of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
. Its fixed points are precisely the closed image of the base-change of the section
\mathfrak{s}^{\theta}:\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}\rightarrow\tilde{Z}^{\theta}
.

5.4.
Smooth localizations of the Drinfeld compactification

In this section, we will compile the results of the previous section in order to obtain some nice cohomologically smooth local descriptions of the Drinfeld compactifications in terms of Zastava spaces.

We start with the following lemma, which shows that base-changing
\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\to\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
along
\ast\to\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
does not change the geometry much. Recall that this base change is denoted
\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}
.

Lemma 5.4.1
.

For all
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
, there exists an Artin
v
-stack
T
admitting cohomologically smooth surjections
T\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}
and
T\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}
such that the pullbacks of
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}
and
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}
to
T
are isomorphic, compatibly with their stratifications.

In fact, one can arrange that
T^{\mathrm{sm}}:=T\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}
still surjects onto
\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}
.

Proof.

It suffices to find a cohomologically smooth Artin
v
-stack
T_{0}
with a cohomologically smooth surjective map
T_{0}\to\mathrm{Bun}_{M}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
such that the corresponding
M
-bundle on
X_{T_{0}}
is trivial in a neighborhood of the Cartier divisor corresponding to the map
T_{0}\to\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
. Indeed, the data parametrized by
\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\to\mathrm{Bun}_{M}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
and
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\to\mathrm{Bun}_{M}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
(as well as conditions defining the stratifications) depend only on the
M
-bundle on the Fargues-Fontaine curve in a neighborhood of the Cartier divisor. Thus, if we define

T=T_{0}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}},

then
T
is also isomorphic to the base change
T_{0}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}
, so has a natural map to
\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}
, and the base changes of the Zastava spaces are also isomorphic. With this choice,
T^{\mathrm{sm}}=T\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}
will continue to surject onto
\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}
(as
T_{0}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}
continues to surject onto
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
).

To construct
T_{0}
, we can use Beauville-Laszlo gluing as in
[
FS21
, Theorem IV.1.19]
, to reduce to finding a cohomologically smooth Artin
v
-stack
T_{1}
with a cohomologically smooth surjective map
T_{1}\to[\ast/M(E)]
such that the associated
M
-bundle on
X_{T_{1}}
is trivial away from a Cartier divisor given by a map
T_{1}\to\mathrm{Div}^{d}
. (Then on
T_{1}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
, the locus where the two Cartier divisors are disjoint will surject onto
T_{1}
.) This can be reduced to
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
, and in this case one can take
T_{1}
to be the open subset of the space parametrizing fibrewise trivial rank
n
bundles
\mathcal{E}
on
X_{S}
together with
n
global sections of
\mathcal{E}(1)
where the resulting map
\mathcal{O}_{X_{S}}^{n}\to\mathcal{E}(1)
is generically an isomorphism.
∎

By combining this with Corollary
5.1.7
, we obtain the following result which will be used in the sequel.

Corollary 5.4.2
.

For all
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
, the moduli spaces
Z^{\theta}
and
Z^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}
are cohomologically smooth.

We fix
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
. For
\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta
we consider the cohomologically smooth map

\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\rightarrow\phantom{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}.

Using the previous Lemma, we have a
v
-stack
T
surjecting onto
\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta^{\prime}}_{M}
and
\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}
and an isomorphism

\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}\times_{\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}}T\simeq\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\times_{\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}}T.

In summary, we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}\times_{\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}}T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bun}_{M},}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{M}^{\leftarrow}}

where the top horizontal arrows are cohomologically smooth.

We let
T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}\rightarrow T
be the base-change of the étale map
(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)})_{\mathrm{disj}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime})}
along
T\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta^{\prime}}_{M}\to\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime})}
. By the factorization property, we also have an open immersion

\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta^{\prime}}_{M}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime})}}(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)})_{\mathrm{disj}}\simeq(\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta})_{\mathrm{disj}}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}.

Note that the map
T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta^{\prime}}_{M}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime})}}(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)})_{\mathrm{disj}}
is cohomologically smooth and hence so is the composition
T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}
with the previous étale map. We obtain a diagram as follows.

(1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}\times_{\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}}T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bun}_{M}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}}

Finally, we get the following critical uniformization result for
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
.

Proposition 5.4.3
.

For all
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
, the map

\bigsqcup_{\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta}(\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}\rightarrow\phantom{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}

is cohomologically smooth and surjective.

Proof.

We already know that it is cohomologically smooth, so we have to see surjectivity. Let us write this surjectivity in terms that are independent of the choice of
T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}
. Namely, we have the locus inside

\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{Z}^{\theta^{\prime}}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime})}}(\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}\times\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta^{\prime}-\theta)})_{\mathrm{disj}}

where the locus of indeterminacy of
\beta_{M}
is concentrated on the part of the Cartier divisor corresponding to the summand parametrized by
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
. This space is actually given by

(\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}})_{\mathrm{disj}},

using the factorization structure of the Zastava spaces. There is a natural surjection

(\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}\to(\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}})_{\mathrm{disj}}

and we have to see that the map

\bigsqcup_{\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta}(\tilde{Z}^{\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}})_{\mathrm{disj}}\to\phantom{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}

is surjective.

By induction on
\theta
, it suffices to show that the subspace
\phantom{}{}_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
lies in the image. To do this, we use Proposition
5.1.8
to see that the space
\bigsqcup_{\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta}Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}
uniformizes the open subspace
\mathrm{Bun}_{P}\subset\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
. In fact, given any fixed point of
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
, even the locus of
\bigsqcup_{\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta}Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\mathrm{sm}}}
where the Cartier divisor is disjoint from the fixed point of
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
will surject. Thus, we see that
\phantom{}{}_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}\simeq\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\mathrm{Bun}_{P}
lies in the image, as desired.
∎

The upper left corner of (
1
) also sits in the following set of Cartesian diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Z}^{\theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{M}\times\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}_{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\square}

where as noted before the bottom horizontal arrow is cohomologically smooth. Therefore, since
Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta}
is cohomologically smooth, we have a cohomologically smooth surjection

(\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}\rightarrow\tilde{Z}^{\theta}

As a consequence of the above discussion, we have shown the following, which says that
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}
is a local model for the singularities of the open subspace
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
of our Drinfeld compactification.

Proposition 5.4.4
.

There exists a
v
-stack
X
with cohomologically smooth surjections onto
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}
and
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
such that for
\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta
the pullbacks of the strata
\phantom{}{}_{\theta^{\prime}}\tilde{Z}^{\theta}
and
\phantom{}{}_{\theta^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
to
X
agree.

Namely, we set

X=\bigsqcup_{\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta}(\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}.

One issue with this proposition is that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Z}^{\theta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{M}^{\leftarrow}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}}

does not commute, so the proposition is not suitable to study the geometry of the map
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}
. However, this can be remedied by working with
X
directly (and remembering its correct map to
\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
).

For our applications, we need to endow
\tilde{Z}^{\theta}
with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action, which it only acquires after base-changing along the étale covering
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
, as in Corollary
5.3.2
.

Thus, we replace the
X
defined above with its base-change along the étale map
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
, and redefine

X:=\bigsqcup_{\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta}((\tilde{Z}^{\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}.

Moreover, set

S:=\bigsqcup_{\theta^{\prime}\geq\theta}((\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}}\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta),\prime}.

Then if we write
\pi:X\rightarrow S
for the natural map, together with its section
i:S\rightarrow X
obtained as the base-change of
\tilde{\mathfrak{s}}^{\theta}
, we have a commutative diagram of the form

(2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bun}_{M},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\mathfrak{q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

where specifically
q
is the precomposition of

(\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times Z^{\theta^{\prime}-\theta})\times_{\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta}\times\mathrm{Mod}_{M}^{+,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}}T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}\rightarrow T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Bun}_{M},

with the étale covering induced by the base-change of
\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}_{\mathcal{Y}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Div}^{(\theta)}
.
Here the map
T_{\theta,\theta^{\prime}-\theta}\rightarrow\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
is the bottom arrow in the diagram (
1
).

We consider the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
X
which only acts on the first factor
\tilde{Z}^{\theta,\prime}
, and is trivial on the other factors. Taking the trivial
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
S
, this makes
\pi
and
i
equivariant.

Summarizing the above discussion, we finally arrive at the following result, which is the key geometric result in our proof of the ULA property for
\tilde{j}_{P!}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}}
.

Corollary 5.4.5
.

For all
\theta
, there exists a diagram as in (
2
) such that the following is true.

(1)

The map
p
is surjective and cohomologically smooth.

(2)

The map
i
is a section of
\pi
which maps
S
isomorphically onto the closed substack given by the preimage along
p
of the closed substack
j_{\theta}:\phantom{}_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}\hookrightarrow\phantom{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
.

(3)

There exists a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
X
lying over the trivial action on
S
which is totally attracting towards the closed section
i
. The map
\pi
is a partially proper
!
-able map of small v-stacks that is representable in locally spatial diamonds.

(4)

The
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
X
preserves the preimages under
p
of the strata
\phantom{}{}_{\theta^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}}
for
\theta^{\prime}\leq\theta
.

We are now finally in a position to prove the ULA theorem.

6.
The ULA theorem

We will now show that
\widetilde{j}_{P!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
is ULA with respect to
\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}
. This will be accomplished by combining the geometry of the Zastava spaces considered in the previous section with some new results on ULA sheaves. Roughly speaking, the key idea here is that in the presence of a contracting
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action, the ULA property automatically extends from the complement of the fixed locus. Our first task is to make this idea precise.

6.1.
ULA magic

In the following, we make use of certain results from
[
FS21
]
proved in the context of the
\ell
-adic
6
-functor formalism. However, these are general results valid for any good
6
-functor formalism, and the results extend verbatim to the motivic formalism used in this paper for general
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
-algebras; details will appear in
[
Sch
]
.

Lemma 6.1.1

(The contraction principle)
.

Let
\pi:X\to S
be a partially proper
!
-able map of small v-stacks that is representable in locally spatial diamonds, and equipped with a section
i:S\to X
and a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action which is totally attracting towards the section. If
A\in D(X,\Lambda)
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant, then
\pi_{!}A\cong i^{!}A
.

Proof.

Special case of (the proof of) hyperbolic localization, more precisely of
[
FS21
, Proposition VI.6.6]
(cf. also the proof of
[
FS21
, Theorem IV.6.5]
).
∎

Theorem 6.1.2
.

Let
\pi:X\to S
be a partially proper
!
-able map that is representable in locally spatial diamonds, and equipped with a section
i:S\to X
and a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action which is totally attracting towards the section. Let
X^{\circ}=X\smallsetminus S
with
j:X^{\circ}\to X
the natural open immersion, and set
\pi^{\circ}=\pi\circ j
. If
A\in D(X^{\circ},\Lambda)
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant and
\pi^{\circ}
-ULA, then
j_{!}A
is
\pi
-ULA.

As a formal consequence, we get that also
j_{\ast}A
is
\pi
-ULA, and that
i^{\ast}j_{\ast}A\in D(S,\Lambda)
is dualizable. We also deduce that if
B\in D(X,\Lambda)
is any
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant sheaf, then it is
\pi
-ULA iff
j^{\ast}B
is
\pi^{\circ}
-ULA and
i^{\ast}B
is dualizable.

Proof.

Let
p_{1},p_{2}:X\times_{S}X\to X
be the evident projections. We need to see that the natural map

p_{1}^{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/S}(j_{!}A)\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}j_{!}A\overset{\alpha}{\to}R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(p_{1}^{\ast}j_{!}A,p_{2}^{!}j_{!}A)

is an isomorphism. Over
X^{\circ}\times_{S}X
this follows from the fact that
A\boxtimes 1
is ULA for
X^{\circ}\times_{S}X\to X
. Similarly over
X\times_{S}X^{\circ}
(argue as in the proof of
[
HS23
, Lemma 4.3]
). So the cone of this map is supported on
(i\times i):S\hookrightarrow X\times_{S}X
. Thus it suffices to see the cone is killed by
(i\times i)^{!}
. Applying the previous lemma to
X\times_{S}X
with the diagonal
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action, we get that
(i\times i)^{!}=(\pi\times\pi)_{!}
as functors applied to the source or target of
\alpha
. We claim that this functor actually kills both the source and target of
\alpha
, and therefore kills the cone. We first note that
(\pi\times\pi)_{!}
kills the source of
\alpha
. For this, use Künneth to write

(\pi\times\pi)_{!}(p_{1}^{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/S}(j_{!}A)\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}j_{!}A)\cong\pi_{!}\mathbb{D}_{X/S}(j_{!}A)\otimes\pi_{!}j_{!}A.

Now use the fact that

\pi_{!}\mathbb{D}_{X/S}(j_{!}A)=\pi_{!}j_{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X^{\circ}/S}(A)=i^{!}j_{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X^{\circ}/S}(A)=0

where the second equality follows from the previous lemma, and we used the general vanishing
i^{!}j_{*}=0
in the final equality. To see that
(i\times i)^{!}
kills the target of
\alpha
, use the general isomorphism

(i\times i)^{!}R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(p_{1}^{\ast}j_{!}A,p_{2}^{!}j_{!}A)\cong R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}((i\times i)^{\ast}p_{1}^{\ast}j_{!}A,(i\times i)^{!}p_{2}^{!}j_{!}A)

and then observe that
(i\times i)^{\ast}p_{1}^{\ast}j_{!}=i^{*}j_{!}=0
.
∎

For our purposes, we will actually need the following generalization of the previous result.

Theorem 6.1.3
.

Let
\pi:X\to S
be a partially proper
!
-able map of small v-stacks that is representable in locally spatial diamonds, and equipped with a section
i:S\to X
and a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action which is totally attracting towards the section. Let
q:S\rightarrow S^{\prime}
be any
!
-able map of small v-stacks and set
\pi^{\prime}:=q\circ\pi
. Let
X^{\circ}=X\smallsetminus S
with
j:X^{\circ}\to X
the natural open immersion, and set
\pi^{\prime\circ}=\pi^{\prime}\circ j
. If
A\in D(X^{\circ},\Lambda)
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant and
\pi^{\prime\circ}
-ULA, then
j_{!}A
is
\pi^{\prime}
-ULA.

Proof.

Let
p_{1},p_{2}:X\times_{S^{\prime}}X\to X
be the evident projections. We need to see that the natural map

p_{1}^{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/S^{\prime}}(j_{!}A)\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}j_{!}A\overset{\alpha}{\to}R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(p_{1}^{\ast}j_{!}A,p_{2}^{!}j_{!}A)

is an isomorphism. As in the previous proof, it is clear that this map is an isomorphism after pullback along the open immersion
X^{\circ}\times_{S^{\prime}}X\to X\times_{S^{\prime}}X
. It is then enough to see that the source and target of
\alpha
are killed by
i^{\prime!}
, where
i^{\prime}=i\times\mathrm{id}:S\times_{S^{\prime}}X\to X\times_{S^{\prime}}X
is the evident base change of
i
. Write also
p_{S}:S\times_{S^{\prime}}X\to S
and
p_{X}:S\times_{S^{\prime}}X\to X
for the evident projections. For the target of
\alpha
the vanishing is clear, because

\displaystyle i^{\prime!}R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(p_{1}^{\ast}j_{!}A,p_{2}^{!}j_{!}A)

\displaystyle\cong R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(i^{\prime\ast}p_{1}^{\ast}j_{!}A,i^{\prime!}p_{2}^{!}j_{!}A)

\displaystyle\cong R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(p_{S}^{\ast}i^{\ast}j_{!}A,i^{\prime!}p_{2}^{!}j_{!}A)

\displaystyle\cong 0

using
i^{\ast}j_{!}A=0
.

To analyze the source of
\alpha
, we will again use the contraction principle. For this we consider the map
\pi_{X}:X\times_{S^{\prime}}X\to S\times_{S^{\prime}}X
given as the evident base change of
\pi
, so
i^{\prime}
is a section of
\pi_{X}
. Then

\displaystyle i^{\prime!}(p_{1}^{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/S^{\prime}}(j_{!}A)\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}j_{!}A)

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{X!}(p_{1}^{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/S^{\prime}}(j_{!}A)\otimes p_{2}^{\ast}j_{!}A)

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{X!}(p_{1}^{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/S^{\prime}}(j_{!}A)\otimes\pi_{X}^{\ast}p_{X}^{\ast}j_{!}A)

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{X!}p_{1}^{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/S^{\prime}}(j_{!}A)\otimes p_{X}^{\ast}j_{!}A

\displaystyle\cong p_{S}^{\ast}\pi_{!}\mathbb{D}_{X/S^{\prime}}(j_{!}A)\otimes p_{X}^{\ast}j_{!}A

\displaystyle\cong p_{S}^{\ast}\pi_{!}j_{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X^{\circ}/S^{\prime}}(A)\otimes p_{X}^{\ast}j_{!}A

\displaystyle\cong p_{S}^{\ast}i^{!}j_{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X^{\circ}/S^{\prime}}(A)\otimes p_{X}^{\ast}j_{!}A

\displaystyle\cong 0

where the first isomorphism follows from the contraction principle, the second isomorphism from
p_{2}=p_{X}\circ\pi_{X}
, the third isomorphism from the projection formula, the fourth isomorphism from proper base change, the fifth isomorphism from the general commutation of Verdier duality, the sixth isomorphism from another application of the contraction principle, and the final vanishing from
i^{!}j_{\ast}=0
.
∎

We also have the following in the previous situation, which is a variant of
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.6.14]
.

Lemma 6.1.4
.

In the situation of Theorem
6.1.3
, let
A\in D(X,\Lambda)
be any sheaf which is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant and
\pi^{\prime}
-ULA. Then
i^{\ast}A
and
i^{!}A\in D(S,\Lambda)
are
q^{\prime}
-ULA.

Proof.

For the first claim, it is equivalent to see that
i_{\ast}i^{\ast}A
is
\pi^{\prime}
-ULA. This follows from the distinguished triangle

j_{!}j^{\ast}A\to A\to i_{\ast}i^{\ast}A\to,

using that
j_{!}j^{\ast}A
is
\pi^{\prime}
-ULA by the previous theorem. The second claim follows from the first claim by writing

\displaystyle i^{!}A

\displaystyle\cong i^{!}\mathbb{D}_{X/S^{\prime}}^{2}A

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{D}_{S/S^{\prime}}i^{\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X/S^{\prime}}A

and invoking the preservation of ULA sheaves under relative Verdier duality, using Verdier biduality for ULA sheaves to justify the first line.
∎

For later use, we record some additional properties of ULA sheaves we will need.

Lemma 6.1.5
.

Consider a diagram
X\overset{j}{\to}\overline{X}\overset{\overline{q}}{\to}S
where
j
is an open immersion and
\overline{q}
is a
!
-able map of small v-stacks. Set
q=\overline{q}\circ j
. Suppose we are given a sheaf
A\in D(X,\Lambda)
such that
j_{!}\mathbb{D}_{X/S}(A)
is
\overline{q}
-ULA (hence so is its Verdier dual
j_{\ast}A
). Then
\overline{q}^{\ast}(-)\otimes j_{\ast}A\cong j_{\ast}(q^{\ast}(-)\otimes A)
.

Proof.

Set
A^{\prime}=\mathbb{D}_{X/S}(A)
. Then, using
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.19]
twice,

\displaystyle\overline{q}^{\ast}(-)\otimes j_{\ast}A

\displaystyle\cong\overline{q}^{\ast}(-)\otimes\mathbb{D}_{\overline{X}/S}(j_{!}A^{\prime})

\displaystyle\cong R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(j_{!}A^{\prime},\overline{q}^{!}(-))

\displaystyle\cong j_{*}R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(A^{\prime},q^{!}(-))

\displaystyle\cong j_{*}(q^{\ast}(-)\otimes\mathbb{D}_{X/S}(A^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\cong j_{*}(q^{\ast}(-)\otimes A),

which gives the result.
∎

Lemma 6.1.6
.

Suppose given a diagram
X_{1}\overset{f_{1}}{\leftarrow}Y\overset{f_{2}}{\rightarrow}X_{2}
and a sheaf
K\in D(Y,\Lambda)
which is
f_{2}
-ULA and whose support is relatively proper over
X_{1}
. Then the functor
F(-)=f_{2!}(K\otimes f_{1}^{\ast}-)
preserves compact objects.

By "relatively proper support" we mean that there is an isomorphism
K\simeq i_{\ast}L
where
i:Y^{\prime}\to Y
is a closed immersion such that
f_{1}\circ i
is proper.

Proof.

It is formal that
F
has right adjoint given by
G(-)=f_{1\ast}R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(K,f_{2}^{!}-)
. Using the ULA hypothesis, this can be rewritten as
f_{1\ast}(\mathbb{D}_{Y/X_{2}}(K)\otimes f_{2}^{\ast}-)
. Using the relatively proper support hypothesis, this can be rewritten as
f_{1!}(\mathbb{D}_{Y/X_{2}}(K)\otimes f_{2}^{\ast}-)
. Now it is clear that
G
commutes with all direct sums, so its left adjoint necessarily preserves compact objects.
∎

6.2.
Proof of the ULA theorem

Thanks to our labor in the previous sections, we can finally conclude our main geometric result.

Theorem 6.2.1
.

The sheaf
\tilde{j}_{P!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
is ULA with respect to
\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}
, and, for all
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
the sheaf
\tilde{j}_{\theta}^{!}\tilde{j}_{P!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
is ULA with respect to the composition

\tilde{j}_{\theta}:\phantom{}_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}\xrightarrow{\tilde{j}_{\theta}}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}\xrightarrow{\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}}\mathrm{Bun}_{M}.

Similarly, the sheaf
\tilde{j}_{P*}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
is ULA with respect to
\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}
, and, for all
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
, the sheaf
\tilde{j}_{\theta}^{*}\tilde{j}_{P*}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
is ULA with respect to
\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}\circ\tilde{j}_{\theta}
.

Proof.

The second claim follows from the first by applying
[
FS21
, Corollary IV.2.25]
.

We have the presentation
\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}}\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}=\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
as an increasing union of open substacks where the transition maps are dictated by the Bruhat ordering on
\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
. Using this, we reduce to showing that, for all
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
, the sheaf
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{j}_{!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
is ULA with respect to
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{q}}\circ\phantom{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{j}
, where
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{j}:\mathrm{Bun}_{P}\hookrightarrow\phantom{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
is the natural open immersion and that
\tilde{j}_{\theta}^{!}\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{j}_{!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
is ULA with respect to
\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}\circ\tilde{j}_{\theta}
. We will do this by induction on the Bruhat ordering.

The base case (
\theta=0
) follows from combining
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.33]
,
[
FS21
, Corollary IV.2.25]
, Proposition
2.1.2
, and Corollary
2.1.6
. For the inductive step, we can assume that
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{j}_{!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
is ULA with over the open substack
\phantom{}{}_{<\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
given by the complement of
\phantom{}{}_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}\subset\phantom{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
, and we want to show it is ULA over all of
\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
. For this, we use the diagram constructed in Corollary
5.4.5
. By Corollary
5.4.5
(1) and
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.13 (ii)]
, we deduce that it suffices to show that
p^{*}\phantom{}_{\leq\theta}\tilde{j}_{!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
is ULA with respect to
q\circ\pi
. By
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.13 (ii)]
, our inductive hypothesis, and Corollary
5.4.5
(2), we know that this is true away from the closed image of the section
i
, with notation as in
5.4.5
. The desired claim now follows by combining Corollary
5.4.5
(3)-(4) with Theorem
6.1.3
, which we apply with
S^{\prime}=\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
. Now the ULAness of
\tilde{j}_{\theta}^{!}\phantom{}{}_{\leq\theta}j_{!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
with respect to
\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}\circ\tilde{j}_{\theta}
follows by applying Lemma
6.1.4
.
∎

Remark 6.2.2
.

We note that the constant sheaf
\Lambda
on
\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
is ULA over
\ast
. In particular, if we have sheaves like
j_{!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
and
\tilde{j}_{\theta}^{!}j_{!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
which are ULA over
\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
then it follows that they are also ULA over the point by
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.26]
.

Before moving into applications, we will now remove our running assumption that the derived group of
G
is simply connected, so that we may conclude the most general possible results for the geometric Eisenstein functors.

6.3.
Fixing the center

In Sections 5.3-7.2, we had a running assumption that the derived group of
G
is simply connected. For the applications in the next section, we will want to remove this assumption. To do this, we consider a variant of the Drinfeld compactification that has the correct properties when the derived group of
G
is not simply connected, partially modelled on the analysis in
[
Sch15
, Section 7]
.

From now on, we let
G
denote a general quasi-split connected reductive group and let

0\rightarrow Z\rightarrow\tilde{G}\rightarrow G\rightarrow 0

be a central extension, where
\tilde{G}
has simply connected derived group.

We now consider a parabolic subgroup
P\subset G
, and write
\tilde{P}\subset\tilde{G}
for the pre-image under the previous map. We consider the induced map

\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{G}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Bun}_{G}.

We note, by
[
FS21
, Lemma III.2.10]
and the proceeding discussion that the Picard stack
\mathrm{Bun}_{Z}
acts on
\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{G}}
by tensoring, and the quotient stack is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Bun}_{G}
. Similarly, we can define an action of
\mathrm{Bun}_{Z}
on
\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{\tilde{P}}
by tensoring the triple
(\mathcal{F}_{G},\mathcal{F}_{M},\tilde{\kappa}_{P})
by the
Z
-bundle. This allows us to define the following.

Definition 6.3.1
.

We define
\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
to be the quotient of
\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{\tilde{P}}
under the action of
\mathrm{Bun}_{Z}
described above.

A priori, this depends on the choice of
\tilde{G}
, but the category of such choices is naturally cofiltered, with transition maps inducing isomorphisms.

This space sits in the following Cartesian diagrams.

Proposition 6.3.2
.

There are a set of Cartesian diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{\tilde{P}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bun}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bun}_{G}}
.
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{j}_{\tilde{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{\tilde{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{j}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{P}}

Proof.

The fact that the right most diagram is Cartesian follows from the definition and the fact that
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{\tilde{P}}
is equivariant for the action of
\mathrm{Bun}_{Z}
. Similarly, we can see that
\tilde{j}_{\tilde{P}}:\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{P}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{\tilde{P}}
is equivariant for the natural
\mathrm{Bun}_{Z}
action on the source. It remains to identify
\mathrm{Bun}_{P}
with the quotient under this action. This is true for the natural induced map on Levi factors
\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{L}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Bun}_{L}
by another application of
[
FS21
, Lemma III.2.10]
. To see it for the parabolics, we can combine
[
FS21
, Lemma III.2.10]
with
[
HI23
, Lemma 3.3]
noting that the
L^{\mathrm{ad}}=\tilde{L}^{\mathrm{ad}}
and that the natural maps
\tilde{L}\rightarrow L\rightarrow L^{\mathrm{ad}}
are all central extensions.
∎

We can use this result to deduce the following properties for the the stack
\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
.

Proposition 6.3.3
.

For
G
a general quasi-split connected reductive group, the following is true.

(1)

There is a naturally defined small Artin v-stack
\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
sitting in a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{p}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{j}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{q}_{P}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{P}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Bun}_{G}}
\textstyle{\text{}\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}

where
\tilde{j}_{P}
is an open immersion. The map
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{P}
is partially proper and representable in locally spatial diamonds, and of locally finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
.

(2)

If
\mathrm{Bun}_{M}^{\alpha}\subset\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
is any connected component with open-closed preimage
\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}^{\alpha}
, the induced map
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{P}^{\alpha}:\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}^{\alpha}\to\mathrm{Bun}_{G}
is proper.

(3)

The stack
\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
admits a locally closed stratification by certain strata
\phantom{}{}_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
, indexed by
\theta\in\Lambda_{G,P}^{\mathrm{pos}}
. Each stratum has a canonical fiber product decomposition

\phantom{}{}_{\theta}\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}\cong\mathrm{Bun}_{P}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}},

where
\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}
is defined exactly as in the case where
G
has simply connected derived group.

(4)

The sheaves
\tilde{j}_{P!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
and
\tilde{j}_{P\ast}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
are
\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}
-ULA.

Proof.

Since the map
\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{G}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Bun}_{G}
is a surjective map of
v
-stacks, using Proposition
6.3.2
we deduce that
\tilde{j}_{P}
is an open immersion by
[
Sch17
, Proposition 10.11 (2)]
and the analogous claim for
\tilde{j}_{\tilde{P}}
. It is clear that the map
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{P}
is partially proper, and it is representable in locally spatial diamonds, and of locally finite
\mathrm{dim.trg}
by
[
FS21
, Proposition 13.4 (v)]
. It then follows that
\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
is Artin
v
-stack using that
\mathrm{Bun}_{G}
is Artin and
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.18 (iii)]

If we fix an element
\tilde{\alpha}\in\pi_{1}(\tilde{M})_{\Gamma}
mapping to
\pi_{1}(M)_{\Gamma}
with images
\tilde{\alpha}_{G}\in\pi_{1}(\tilde{G})_{\Gamma}
and
\alpha_{G}\in\pi_{1}(G)_{\Gamma}
, respectively, then Proposition
6.3.2
implies that we have a Cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{\tilde{P}}^{\tilde{\alpha}_{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{G}}^{\tilde{\alpha}_{G}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}^{\alpha_{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Bun}_{G}^{\alpha_{G}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{P}^{\tilde{\alpha}_{M}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{P}^{\alpha_{M}}}

where the vertical maps are torsors under the neutral component of
\mathrm{Bun}_{Z}
, and in particular are surjections of
v
-stacks. Since the bottom map is proper, the top map is also proper using
[
Sch17
, Proposition 10.11 (o)]
. This establishes (1)-(2). For (3), we note that the locally closed immersion

\tilde{j}_{\theta,\tilde{P}}:\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{M}}}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{M}}}\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{P}}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{\tilde{P}}

is equivariant for the natural action of
\mathrm{Bun}_{Z}
on source and target and thus descends to a map

\tilde{j}_{\theta,P}:\mathrm{Mod}^{+,\theta}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\times_{\mathrm{Bun}_{M}}\mathrm{Bun}_{P}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}

which is also a locally closed immersion by using
[
Sch17
, Proposition 10.11 (i)]
.

It remains to show the ULA claim. It suffices to establish the claim for
\tilde{j}_{P!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}})
by
[
FS21
, Corollary IV.2.25]
. Since ULA can be checked after a smooth surjective pullback, by applying proper base-change to the left Cartesian diagram in Proposition
6.3.2
, we reduce to showing that
\tilde{j}_{\tilde{P}!}(\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{P}}})
is ULA over
\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
, where we know that it is ULA over
\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{M}}
by Theorem
6.2.1
. Therefore, by
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.26]
the claim reduces to the following lemma.

Lemma 6.3.4
.

The constant sheaf
\Lambda
on
\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{M}}
is ULA with respect to the map
\mathrm{Bun}_{\tilde{M}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
.

Proof.

By choosing a
v
-cover of
\mathrm{Bun}_{M}
trivializing the
\mathrm{Bun}_{Z}
-torsor and using
[
FS21
, Proposition IV.2.25]
, we reduce to showing that the constant sheaf on
\mathrm{Bun}_{Z}
is ULA over
\ast
, and this is clear.
∎

∎

Remark 6.3.5
.

One could of course perform the analogous constructions and deduce the analogous properties for the compactification
\overline{\mathrm{Bun}}_{P}
.

7.
Applications

7.1.
Finiteness theorems

Let
G
be any reductive group over
E
.

Theorem 7.1.1
.

Fix a parabolic
P=MU\subset G
, and let
\Lambda
be any
\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}[\sqrt{q}]
-algebra. Let
\alpha\in\pi_{0}(\mathrm{Bun}_{M})
be any element.

(1)

The functors
\mathrm{Eis}_{P!}^{\alpha}
and
\mathrm{Eis}_{P\ast}^{\alpha}
preserve ULA objects.

(2)

The functor
\mathrm{CT}_{P!}^{\alpha}\cong\mathrm{CT}_{P^{-}\ast}^{\alpha}
preserves compact objects.

Proof.

By Theorem
6.2.1
and its extension in Proposition
6.3.3
(5), the sheaves
\tilde{j}^{\alpha}_{P!}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}^{\alpha}}
and
\tilde{j}^{\alpha}_{P\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}^{\alpha}}
are
\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}^{\alpha}
-ULA. For (1), we then note the isomorphisms

\mathrm{Eis}_{P!}^{\alpha}(A)\cong\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\alpha}_{P!}(\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}^{\alpha\ast}A\otimes\tilde{j}^{\alpha}_{P!}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}^{\alpha}})

and

\mathrm{Eis}_{P\ast}^{\alpha}(A)\cong\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\alpha}_{P!}(\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}^{\alpha\ast}A\otimes\tilde{j}^{\alpha}_{P\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}^{\alpha}})

using Lemma
6.1.5
and the ULA property of
\tilde{j}^{\alpha}_{P\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}^{\alpha}}
for the latter. Then ULA-twisted pullback preserves the ULA condition, and
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\alpha}_{P!}=\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\alpha}_{P\ast}
preserves the ULA condition since
\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{P}^{\alpha}
is proper by
6.3.3
(2). This gives (1).

For (2), we apply Lemma
6.1.6
, taking
f_{1}=\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}_{P}^{\alpha}
,
f_{2}=\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}^{\alpha}
, and
K=\tilde{j}^{\alpha}_{!}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}^{\alpha}}
in the notation of that lemma. The lemma then implies that the functor
F=\mathrm{CT}_{P!}^{\alpha}
preserves compact objects, so
\mathrm{CT}_{P^{-}\ast}^{\alpha}
does as well by second adjointness.
∎

Remark 7.1.2
.

By second adjointness and the basic definitions and adjunctions,
\mathrm{Eis}_{P!}
preserves colimits and
\mathrm{Eis}_{P\ast}
preserves limits while
\mathrm{CT}_{P\ast}\cong\mathrm{CT}_{P^{-}!}
preserves all limits and colimits. After restricting to components, it is also true that
\mathrm{Eis}_{P\ast}^{\alpha}
preserves colimits and
\mathrm{Eis}_{P!}^{\alpha}
preserves limits. The first claim follows from the isomorphism

\mathrm{Eis}_{P\ast}^{\alpha}(-)\cong\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\alpha}_{P!}(\tilde{j}^{\alpha}_{P\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}^{\alpha}}\otimes\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}^{\alpha\ast}-)

established in the previous proof, noting that all functors on the right commute with colimits. The second claim follows from the isomorphism

\mathrm{Eis}_{P!}^{\alpha}(-)\cong\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\alpha}_{P\ast}R\mathscr{H}\mathrm{om}(\tilde{j}^{\alpha}_{P\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}^{\alpha}},\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P}^{\alpha!}-)

which follows by a similar argument, noting that all functors on the right commute with limits.

This also shows that the functor
\mathrm{Eis}_{P!}^{\alpha}(-)
has a left adjoint which preserves compact objects, given explicitly by

\mathrm{CT}_{P?}^{\alpha}=\tilde{\mathfrak{q}}_{P!}^{\alpha}(\tilde{j}^{\alpha}_{P\ast}\mathrm{IC}_{\mathrm{Bun}_{P}^{\alpha}}\otimes\tilde{\mathfrak{p}}^{\alpha\ast}_{P}-).

It is not hard to deduce from second adjointness that Bernstein-Zelevinsky duality exchanges
\mathrm{CT}_{P!}^{\alpha}
and
\mathrm{CT}_{P?}^{\alpha}
. However, we are not aware of any applications of this functor.

Theorem 7.1.3
.

The functor
i_{b\sharp}
preserves ULA objects, and the functor
i_{b}^{!}
preserves compact objects.

Proof.

When
G
is quasisplit, we can let
P=MU
be the dynamic parabolic of the Newton cocharacter
\nu_{b}
. Then, by
2.2.5
(5) up to an irrelevant shift and twist,
i_{b\sharp}
agrees with
\mathrm{Eis}_{P^{-}!}\circ i_{b!}^{M}
, and by Theorem
1.2.1
.(1), this composition of functors preserves the ULA property.

Similarly, by Corollary
2.2.5
(4) up to an irrelevant shift and twist,
i_{b}^{!}
agrees with
i_{b}^{M,\ast}\mathrm{CT}_{P\ast}
, and by Theorem
1.2.1
.(2)-(3), this composition of functors preserves compactness.

Removing the quasisplitness assumption, we can first deduce the case when
G
has connected center, and then the general case by z-extensions.
∎

Theorem 7.1.4
.

If
j:U\to\mathrm{Bun}_{G}
is the inclusion of a quasicompact open substack and
A\in D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\Lambda)
is compact, then
j_{\ast}A
has compact stalks. In particular, if
A\in D(G_{b}(E),\Lambda)
is compact, then
i_{b\ast}A
has compact stalks.

Proof.

The second part follows from the first by factoring
i_{b}
as the composition
\mathrm{Bun}_{G}^{b}\overset{h}{\to}\mathrm{Bun}_{G}^{\preceq b}\overset{j}{\to}
and then writing
i_{b\ast}=j_{\ast}h_{\ast}
, using that
h_{\ast}=h_{!}
preserves compactness.

For the first claim, it is equivalent to prove that for any open immersion
j:U\subset V
of quasicompact open substacks of
\mathrm{Bun}_{G}
, the functor
j_{\ast}
preserves compact objects. By induction, we may assume that
U=V\setminus\{b\}
for some
b\in B(G)
. We need to prove that
i_{b}^{\ast}j_{\ast}
preserves compact objects. But like for any open-closed stratification, we have
i_{b}^{\ast}j_{*}=i_{b}^{!}j_{!}[1]
, and we already know that
j_{!}
and
i_{b}^{!}
preserve compact objects, using the previous theorem for the latter.
∎

7.2.
The cuspidal-Eisenstein decomposition

Proof of Theorem
1.3.2
.

(1) is clear. Indeed, the functors
\mathrm{CT}_{P\ast}
commute with all limits since they are right adjoints, and with all colimits since their left adjoints preserve compact objects. Moreover, by second adjointness they also commute with Verdier duality up to swapping
P
for its opposite. Moreover, if
A
is cuspidal then
i_{b}^{\ast}A=i_{b}^{!}A=0
for all non-basic
b
, since the stalk functors can be obtained from suitable constant term functors, by Corollary
2.2.5
(3)-(4).

(2) is completely formal, since
D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{Eis}}
is stable under all colimits and
D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{cusp}}
is exactly the right orthogonal of
D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\Lambda)^{\mathrm{Eis}}
.

(3) requires some work. The essential point is that for each basic
b
,

D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})^{\mathrm{cusp}}\cap i_{b!}D(G_{b}(E),\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})

identifies with the support of a union of supercuspidal Bernstein components for
G_{b}(E)
, which by (1) is stable under Verdier duality. But then Bernstein-Zelevinsky duality and Verdier duality induce the same involution on the set of Bernstein components, so one deduces that
D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})^{\mathrm{cusp}}
is generated under colimits by objects of
D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})^{\mathrm{cusp}}\cap D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})^{\omega}
, and that
D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})^{\mathrm{cusp}}\cap D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})^{\omega}
is stable under Bernstein-Zelevinsky duality.

Now to conclude, take any
A\in D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})^{\mathrm{cusp}}\cap D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})^{\omega}
. Then for any
P=MU\subset G
and any compact
B\in D(\mathrm{Bun}_{M},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})
, we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\mathrm{RHom}(\mathrm{Eis}_{P!}B,A)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{RHom}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{BZ}}A,\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{BZ}}\mathrm{Eis}_{P!}B)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{RHom}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{BZ}}A,\mathrm{Eis}_{P^{-}!}\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{BZ}}B)

using second adjointness and the preservation of compact objects under
!
-Eisenstein series. Varying
B
and
P
, and using the involutivity of Bernstein-Zelevinsky duality on compact objects together with the previous step, we deduce that
D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})^{\mathrm{cusp}}
is the
left
orthogonal of
D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})^{\mathrm{Eis}}
. Since it is also the right orthogonal by definition, we get the desired orthogonal decomposition.
∎

7.3.
Continuity of gluing functors

We end with an application to representation theory. To put this result into context, let
G
be a connected reductive group over a nonarchimedean local field
E
. Fix an algebraically closed field
C
of characteristic zero. Write
\mathrm{Groth}(G)
for the Grothendieck group of finite-length admissible
C
-representations of
G(E)
. Any element
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G(E),C)
defines a linear form
\mathrm{Groth}(G)\to C
by sending an irreducible representation
\pi
to
\mathrm{tr}(\phi|\pi)
and then extending linearly. By definition, the linear maps
\mathrm{Groth}(G)\to C
of this form are called
trace forms
.

Definition 7.3.1
.

A homomorphism
f:\mathrm{Groth}(G)\to\mathrm{Groth}(H)
is
continuous
if for all trace forms
h
on
\mathrm{Groth}(H)
,
h\circ f
is a trace form on
\mathrm{Groth}(G)
.

This condition turns out to be satisfied by many natural operations appearing in representation theory.

Example 7.3.2
.

The involution on
\mathrm{Groth}(G)
induced by smooth duality is continuous.

Example 7.3.3
.

If
P=MU\subset G
is a parabolic, the maps
[i_{P}^{G}]:\mathrm{Groth}(M)\to\mathrm{Groth}(G)
and
[r_{G}^{P}]:\mathrm{Groth}(G)\to\mathrm{Groth}(M)
induced by parabolic induction and Jacquet module are continuous. For
[i_{P}^{G}]
this follows from van Dijk’s formula, which gives an explicit recipe for a map
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G(E))\mapsto\phi^{P}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(M(E))
such that
\mathrm{tr}(\phi|i_{P}^{G}\pi)=\mathrm{tr}(\phi^{P}|\pi)
.

For
[r_{G}^{P}]
the result follows by induction on the semisimple rank, by combining the trace Paley-Wiener theorem, the geometric lemma, and the result for parabolic induction.

Example 7.3.4
.

The Aubert-Zelevinsky involution on
\mathrm{Groth}(G)
is continuous. This follows, for instance, by writing it as a virtual combination of compositions of parabolic inductions and Jacquet modules, and then using the previous example.

Example 7.3.5
.

If
G=\mathrm{GL}_{n}
and
G^{\prime}
is an inner form of
G
, Badulescu’s "inverse Jacquet-Langlands map"
\mathrm{LJ}:\mathrm{Groth}(G)\to\mathrm{Groth}(G^{\prime})
is continuous.

Example 7.3.6
.

If
(G,\mu,b)
is any local shtuka datum, the map
\mathrm{Mant}_{G,\mu,b}:\mathrm{Groth}(G_{b})\to\mathrm{Groth}(G)
is continuous. This is Theorem 6.5.4 in
[
HKW22
]
.

As an application of our finiteness theorems, we prove the following result.

Theorem 7.3.7
.

For any
b,b^{\prime}\in B(G)
, the map
[i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast}i_{b\sharp}]:\mathrm{Groth}(G_{b})\to\mathrm{Groth}(G_{b^{\prime}})
is continuous.

Note that
i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast}i_{b\sharp}
preserves finite length representations by Theorem
1.2.1
, so this statement is well-posed.

Proof.

It is equivalent, and notationally simpler, to prove this result for the renormalized variant
[i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}]
. We closely follow the proof of
[
HKW22
, Theorem 6.5.4]
. More precisely, choosing an element
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(G_{b^{\prime}}(E))
, we will show that the linear form on
\mathrm{Groth}(G_{b})
given by
\pi\mapsto\mathrm{tr}(\phi\mid i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\pi)
satisfies the conditions of the trace Paley-Wiener theorem, applied to the group
G_{b}(E)
. We refer to loc. cit. for the statement of the trace Paley-Wiener theorem in the form we will use. For the remainder of the proof, we fix a pro-
p
compact open subgroup
K\subset G_{b^{\prime}}(E)
such that
\phi
is bi-
K
-invariant.

Verification of Condition 1.
If
\mathrm{tr}(\phi\mid i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\pi)
is nonzero, then
(i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\pi)^{K}
is nonzero. Therefore, it suffices to see that there is some open compact
J\subset G_{b}(E)
such that
(i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\pi)^{K}\neq 0
only if
\pi^{J}\neq 0
. For this, we compute

\displaystyle(i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\pi)^{K}

\displaystyle\cong R\mathrm{Hom}(i_{b^{\prime}\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\mathrm{ind}_{K}^{G_{b^{\prime}}(E)}\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}},i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\pi)

\displaystyle\cong R\mathrm{Hom}(i_{b!}^{\mathrm{ren}}\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{coh}}\pi,i_{b^{\prime}!}^{\mathrm{ren}}\mathrm{ind}_{K}^{G_{b^{\prime}}(E)}\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})

\displaystyle\cong R\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{coh}}\pi,i_{b}^{!\mathrm{ren}}i_{b^{\prime}!}^{\mathrm{ren}}\mathrm{ind}_{K}^{G_{b^{\prime}}(E)}\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}})

\displaystyle\cong R\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{coh}}i_{b}^{!\mathrm{ren}}i_{b^{\prime}!}^{\mathrm{ren}}\mathrm{ind}_{K}^{G_{b^{\prime}}(E)}\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}},\pi)

where the second line follows from Bernstein-Zelevinsky duality and the isomorphism
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{BZ}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\cong\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{coh}}i_{b!}^{\mathrm{ren}}
(applied also for
b^{\prime}
), and in passing to the fourth line we crucially used that
i_{b}^{!\mathrm{ren}}
preserves compact objects, by Theorem
7.1.3
. Now
\mathbb{D}_{\mathrm{coh}}i_{b}^{!\mathrm{ren}}i_{b^{\prime}!}^{\mathrm{ren}}\mathrm{ind}_{K}^{G_{b^{\prime}}(E)}\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}
is compact, so it is supported on finitely many Bernstein components for
G_{b}(E)
. This shows that the irreducible
\pi
’s with
(i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\pi)^{K}\neq 0
are supported on finitely many Bernstein components for
G_{b}(E)
. The existence of the desired open compact
J
is then immediate.

Verification of Condition 2.
Fix
P=MU\subset G_{b}
and
\sigma
an irreducible smooth
M(E)
-representation. Let
X=\mathrm{Spec}R
be the smooth affine algebraic variety over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}}
parametrizing unramified characters of
M(E)
. Let
\boldsymbol{\psi}:M(E)\to R^{\times}
be the universal character, and form
\Pi=i_{P}^{G_{b}}(\sigma\boldsymbol{\psi})
. This is an admissible smooth
R[G_{b}(E)]
-module interpolating the parabolic inductions
i_{P}^{G_{b}}(\sigma\psi)
for varying unramified characters
\psi
. Since
\Pi
is admissible, the pushforward
i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\Pi\in D(\mathrm{Bun}_{G},R)
is ULA by Theorem
7.1.3
, so then also
i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\Pi\in D(G_{b^{\prime}}(E),R)
is ULA, and in particular
(i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}\Pi)^{K}
is a perfect complex of
R
-modules. The element
\phi
naturally defines an endomorphism of this perfect complex, and its trace
f\in R
interpolates the individual traces
\mathrm{tr}(\phi\mid i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}i_{P}^{G_{b}}(\sigma\psi))
as
\psi
varies. This shows that
\mathrm{tr}(\phi\mid i_{b^{\prime}}^{\ast\mathrm{ren}}i_{b\sharp}^{\mathrm{ren}}i_{P}^{G_{b}}(\sigma\psi))
is an algebraic function of
\psi
, as desired.
∎
